Stress Physiology: Fight-or-Flight, SAM, and HPA

Same Concept, New FramingStress Management9 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
Stress is a state of worry or mental tension caused by a difficult situation and is a natural human response to challenges and threats.
Examiner Pattern: The clinical or theoretical framing changes, but the underlying rule stays the same.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale
2028 Q7
PDF p. 2
Which stress response pathway is associated with the “fight-or-flight” reaction?
A. Parasympathetic-Vagal Axis
B. Limbic-Hippocampal Axis
C. Sympathetic-Adrenal-Medullary (SAM) Axis ✓
D. Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) Axis
C
A — Incorrect
Parasympathetic activation promotes relaxation. Slow diaphragmatic breathing lowers heart rate and blood pressure through parasympathetic activity.Source basis: Stress p.3
B — Incorrect
Sympathetic-Adrenal-Medullary (SAM) AxisSource basis: Original uploaded exam rationale
C — Correct
The SAM axis mediates the fast fight-or-flight response: hypothalamus activates the sympathetic system, the adrenal medulla releases epinephrine and norepinephrine, and heart rate, blood pressure, glucose, and alertness rise.Source basis: Stress p.1
D — Incorrect
The HPA axis is the slower sustained stress pathway: CRH → ACTH → adrenal cortical glucocorticoids, mainly cortisol.Source basis: Stress p.1
Original PDF Rationale
Sympathetic-Adrenal-Medullary (SAM) Axis
● “Fight or flight” (fast response)
● Hypothalamus → activates sympathetic nervous system
● Adrenal medulla releases:
○ Epinephrine (adrenaline)
○ Norepinephrine (noradrenaline)
● Effects: ↑ HR, NP, glucose, alertness
Reference trans/book: 2028 TRANS - [PSY - LE 1] 03 - Stress and
Stress Management
2028 Q8
PDF p. 2
Which stress response pathway involves a slower, sustained reaction?
A. Sympathetic-Adrenal-Medullary (SAM) axis
B. Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) axis ✓
C. Reticular Formation Pathway
D. Autonomic Nervous Loop
B
A — Incorrect
The SAM axis mediates the fast fight-or-flight response: hypothalamus activates the sympathetic system, the adrenal medulla releases epinephrine and norepinephrine, and heart rate, blood pressure, glucose, and alertness rise.Source basis: Stress p.1
B — Correct
The HPA axis is the slower sustained stress pathway: CRH → ACTH → adrenal cortical glucocorticoids, mainly cortisol.Source basis: Stress p.1
C — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) axis: The HPA axis is the slower sustained stress pathway: CRH → ACTH → adrenal cortical glucocorticoids, mainly cortisol.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
D — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) axis: The HPA axis is the slower sustained stress pathway: CRH → ACTH → adrenal cortical glucocorticoids, mainly cortisol.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
Original PDF Rationale
Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) Axis
Stressor → hypothalamus releases corticotropin-releasing hormone
(CRH) → prompts pituitary gland to release adrenocorticotropic hormone
(ACTH) → Glucocorticoids (Cortisol)
CORTISOL helps the body to cope with prolonged or chronic stress by:
● Mobilizing energy stores
● Suppress inflammation and immune responses so it would be
able to support the body in terms of adaptation to challenges

2027 Q42
PDF p. 5
In this stage, humans exhibit a “fight or flight” response, as a reaction to stress.
A. Stage I ✓
B. Stage II
C. Stage III
D. Stage IV
A
A — Correct
Alarm is the first GAS stage and includes the immediate fight-or-flight response.Source basis: 2027 exam Q42
B — Incorrect
Resistance is the GAS stage in which the body adapts to continuing stressors and attempts to reduce their effects.Source basis: 2027 exam Q43
C — Incorrect
Exhaustion occurs after prolonged stress depletes adaptive resources, increasing physical, psychological, and interpersonal consequences.Source basis: 2027 exam Q44
D — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Stage I: Alarm is the first GAS stage and includes the immediate fight-or-flight response.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
Original PDF Rationale
Stage I: Alarm Reaction
● Immediate response to a stressor, causing anxiety, fear, shock, or
confusion.
● "Fight or flight" response prepares the body for action but weakens the
immune system, making one susceptible to illness.
● Also called the self-correcting stage.
● Also called the self-correcting stage.
Stage II: Resistance (Adaptation)
● Body adapts to ongoing stress, characterized by aggression, withdrawal,
or repression.
● Changes occur to minimize the stressor's effects. For instance, in
starvation, physical activity may decrease, and nutrient absorption may
increase.
increase.
Stage III: Exhaustion
● Prolonged stress leads to physical (headaches, flu), psychological
(depression), and interpersonal (relationship breakdown) effects.
● The body’s defenses collapse, with reduced immunity leading to
vulnerability to serious illnesses like heart attacks or infections.
2027 Q46
PDF p. 5
Which neurotransmitter is released when stressors activate the noradrenergic systems in the brain?
A. Catecholamine ✓
B. Glutamate
C. Peptides
D. Thyroxine
A
A — Correct
Catecholamines are released in the rapid stress response. The SAM pathway involves epinephrine and norepinephrine.Source basis: Stress p.1 and 2027 exam Q46
B — Incorrect
The 2027 exam identifies glutamate as an excitatory neurotransmitter that can modulate stress-response circuitry, but it is not the catecholamine released by the noradrenergic system.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
C — Incorrect
The 2027 exam notes that peptides are chains of amino acids and are not the specific stress hormone asked in the glucocorticoid question.Source basis: 2027 exam Q47 rationale
D — Incorrect
Thyroxine is a thyroid hormone primarily involved in metabolism, not the catecholamine or glucocorticoid response tested in these stress questions.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
Original PDF Rationale
Activation of noradrenergic system (specifically Locus ceruleus) → causes
catecholamine release from ANS
Corticotropin releasing factor, glutamate, GABA generate stress response or
modulate dopaminergic and noradrenergic brain circuitry
Refers to classification of molecules
Primarily involves in regulation of metabolism
2027 Q47
PDF p. 6
Mr. A, while doing his morning walk, was attacked by a big dog. He was surprised he was able to climb a tree very fast to avoid being bitten by the dog. This action is due to what hormone that is released during a stressful situation?
A. Glucocorticoid ✓
B. Glutamate
C. Peptides
D. Thyroxine
A
A — Correct
Glucocorticoids such as cortisol are released through the HPA pathway and mobilize energy while helping the body adapt to prolonged stress.Source basis: Stress p.1 and 2027 exam Q47
B — Incorrect
The 2027 exam identifies glutamate as an excitatory neurotransmitter that can modulate stress-response circuitry, but it is not the catecholamine released by the noradrenergic system.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
C — Incorrect
The 2027 exam notes that peptides are chains of amino acids and are not the specific stress hormone asked in the glucocorticoid question.Source basis: 2027 exam Q47 rationale
D — Incorrect
Thyroxine is a thyroid hormone primarily involved in metabolism, not the catecholamine or glucocorticoid response tested in these stress questions.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
Original PDF Rationale
Glucocorticoids promote energy use
● Pathway of release: stress ➡CRF secretion ➡ACTH release
➡stimulation of adrenal cortex ➡ glucocorticoid release
Excitatory neurotransmitter in the brain
Chain of amino acids that are considered as building blocks of proteins
Type of thyroid hormone

C. Catecholamines

Glucocorticoid increases blood sugar levels, providing the burst of energy needed to
respond to stress.
Peptides are short chains of amino acids
Catecholamines also initiate rapid response, but glucocorticoids are for more
complex adaptive responses such as climbing a tree.

provide the rapid burst of energy during an acute stress situation.

Bandura: Modeling, Self-Efficacy, and Social Learning

Same Concept, New FramingTheories of Personality5 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
Self-efficacy is Bandura’s concept for a person’s belief in their ability to succeed or perform effectively.
Examiner Pattern: The clinical or theoretical framing changes, but the underlying rule stays the same.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale
2028 Q44
PDF p. 11
An emergency room resident starts behaving arrogantly towards the nurses after seeing their consultant act the same way. Which concept from Bandura’s theory explains this?
A. Modelling ✓
B. Negative reinforcement
C. Phallic fixations
D. Reinforcement schedules
A
A — Correct
Bandura’s social-learning approach emphasizes learning by observing and imitating models. The Bobo doll experiment is used in the exam to illustrate observational learning.Source basis: 2028 exam Q44 and 2027 exam Q88
B — Incorrect
Negative reinforcement removes an aversive condition after a behavior, thereby increasing the likelihood of that behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q64 and 2027 exam Q82
C — Incorrect
The exam associates phallic fixation with traits such as vanity, exhibitionism, and pride.Source basis: 2028 exam Q60
D — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Modelling: Bandura’s social-learning approach emphasizes learning by observing and imitating models. The Bobo doll experiment is used in the exam to illustrate observational learning.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
Original PDF Rationale
Reference trans/book: 2028 Trans 5 Theories of Personality

Original PDF Rationale
oAclbceurrtaBgaanindura is a Canadian-American psychologist who rejected the traditional
behaviorist views of personality that presented humans as passive recipients of
environmental stimuli. He argued that there were both internal and external
determinants of behavior, and introduced the concepts of reciprocal determinism
and social learning.
Radical Behaviorism: Operant conditioning is better able to explain behavior, in
that people are manipulated by rewards or punishments of which they are not
always cognizant.
always cognizant.
Five Factor Model (The Big 5): personality is stable, especially after age 30, that it
is universal, and that the core structure consists of five major domains: Openness.
conscientiousness, extraversion, agreeableness, neuroticism.
2027 Q88
PDF p. 11
Which of the following is derived from the Bobo doll experiment?
A. Self efficacy
B. Self Observational learning ✓
C. Reciprocal determination
D. Positive reinforcement
B
A — Incorrect
Self-efficacy is Bandura’s concept for a person’s belief in their ability to succeed or perform effectively.Source basis: 2028 exam Q50
B — Correct
Bandura’s social-learning approach emphasizes learning by observing and imitating models. The Bobo doll experiment is used in the exam to illustrate observational learning.Source basis: 2028 exam Q44 and 2027 exam Q88
C — Incorrect
Bandura’s reciprocal determinism describes interaction among behavior, personal factors, and the environment.Source basis: 2027 exam Q83/Q88
D — Incorrect
Positive reinforcement adds a pleasant stimulus after a behavior to increase the likelihood that the behavior will recur.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55
Original PDF Rationale
Refers to one's belief in their ability to succeed, not directly explored in the Bobo
doll experiment.
In the Bobo doll experiment, the doll was used to demonstrate observational
learning, where children imitated aggressive behaviors they observed being
modeled by adults towards the doll.
Involves the interaction between behavior, personal factors, and environment, but
wasn't the main focus of the experiment.
wasn't the main focus of the experiment.
Relies on rewards to strengthen behavior, whereas the Bobo doll experiment
showed learning through observation without reinforcement.

Big Five Personality Traits

Inverted / Choice VariantsTheories of Personality5 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
Humility is not one of the OCEAN Big Five domains listed by the exams. OCEAN is openness, conscientiousness, extraversion, agreeableness, and neuroticism.
Examiner Pattern: The examiner rotates the same definitions through different stems and answer positions, so learn the whole comparison set rather than one isolated answer.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale
2028 Q46
PDF p. 12
Which of the following is not part of the five factor model?
A. Agreeableness
B. Humility ✓
C. Neuroticism
D. Openness to Experience
B
A — Incorrect
Agreeableness is one of the Big Five traits. The 2028 exam notes that agreeableness and conscientiousness tend to increase with age.Source basis: 2028 exam Q46/Q65
B — Correct
Humility is not one of the OCEAN Big Five domains listed by the exams. OCEAN is openness, conscientiousness, extraversion, agreeableness, and neuroticism.Source basis: 2028 exam Q46 and 2027 exam Q89
C — Incorrect
Neuroticism ranges from sensitive/anxious to secure/confident. Low neuroticism corresponds to greater emotional stability.Source basis: 2028 exam Q52
D — Incorrect
Openness to experience reflects imagination and curiosity versus caution and resistance to change.Source basis: 2028 exam Q52

2028 Q52
PDF p. 13
Which Big Five trait reflects emotional stability?
A. Conscientiousness
B. Extraversion
C. Neuroticism (low level) ✓
D. Openness
C
A — Incorrect
Conscientiousness reflects organization, dependability, and disciplined behavior. High conscientiousness fits consistent planning, punctuality, and careful work.Source basis: 2028 exam Q52/Q57
B — Incorrect
Extraversion ranges from outgoing and energetic to solitary and reserved.Source basis: 2028 exam Q52
C — Correct
Neuroticism ranges from sensitive/anxious to secure/confident. Low neuroticism corresponds to greater emotional stability.Source basis: 2028 exam Q52
D — Incorrect
Openness to experience reflects imagination and curiosity versus caution and resistance to change.Source basis: 2028 exam Q52
Original PDF Rationale
Conscientiousness: organized, dependable vs. careless, easygoing
Extraversion: outgoing, energetic vs. solitary, reserved
Neuroticism: sensitive, anxious vs. secure, confident
A person with stable emotions would score LOW in neuroticism,
meaning they are generally calm, secure, and less likely to be easily
upset.
Openness to experience: imaginative, curious vs cautious, resistant to
change.
Reference trans/book: 2028 Trans 5 Theories of Personality

Freud: Topographic Model — Conscious, Preconscious, Unconscious

Inverted / Choice VariantsTheories of Personality5 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
Preconscious material is not currently in awareness but can be brought into consciousness by shifting attention or being prompted.
Examiner Pattern: The examiner rotates the same definitions through different stems and answer positions, so learn the whole comparison set rather than one isolated answer.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale
2028 Q42
PDF p. 10
Which of the following examples best illustrates a preconscious process?
A. Dreaming of an embarrassing event
B. Feeling irrational fear of spiders without knowing why
C. Solving a complex math problem in your head
D. Suddenly recalling a childhood friend’s name when prompted ✓
D
A — Incorrect
The available project materials identify this choice, but do not provide enough choice-specific detail to expand it reliably.Source basis: Project-source limitation
B — Incorrect
The available project materials identify this choice, but do not provide enough choice-specific detail to expand it reliably.Source basis: Project-source limitation
C — Incorrect
The available project materials identify this choice, but do not provide enough choice-specific detail to expand it reliably.Source basis: Project-source limitation
D — Correct
An unconscious process A conscious process Preconscious: memories that we can bring to consciousness. Conscious: everything we are aware of at the moment; just the “tip of the iceberg.” Unconscious: memories, wishes, and instincts (desires) that are too threatening or painful to bring to consciousness.Source basis: Original uploaded exam rationale
Original PDF Rationale
An unconscious process
An unconscious process
A conscious process
Preconscious: memories that we can bring to consciousness.
Conscious: everything we are aware of at the moment; just the “tip of
the iceberg.”
Unconscious: memories, wishes, and instincts (desires) that are too
threatening or painful to bring to consciousness.
Reference trans/book: 2028 LE1 - TRANS 5: Theories of Personality

Which does not belong to the Topographical Model of the Mind?
A. Conscious mind
B. Pre-conscious mind
C. Pre-unconscious mind ✓
D. Unconscious mind
C
A — Incorrect
Conscious material is what the person is aware of at the present moment.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42
B — Incorrect
Preconscious material is not currently in awareness but can be brought into consciousness by shifting attention or being prompted.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42 and Q68
C — Correct
“Pre-unconscious” is not one of Freud’s topographic divisions. The exam identifies conscious, preconscious, and unconscious as the three levels.Source basis: 2028 exam Q66
D — Incorrect
Freud’s unconscious contains memories, wishes, instincts, and other material that is repressed or difficult to bring into conscious awareness.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42 and Q67
Original PDF Rationale
The Pre-unconscious mind is NOT part of the topographical map of
the mind

2028 Q55
PDF p. 14
A patient admitted to the psychiatric ward fixed his bed and cleaned his personal space in the morning. As a result, the nursing staff gave him an additional 10 minutes of recreational activity in the afternoon. This scenario best illustrates which behavioral principle?
A. Negative Punishment
B. Negative Reinforcement
C. Positive Punishment
D. Positive Reinforcement ✓
D
A — Incorrect
Negative punishment removes a pleasant stimulus in order to decrease a behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55 and 2027 exam Q82
B — Incorrect
Negative reinforcement removes an aversive condition after a behavior, thereby increasing the likelihood of that behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q64 and 2027 exam Q82
C — Incorrect
Positive punishment adds an aversive consequence to decrease a behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55 and 2027 exam Q82
D — Correct
Positive reinforcement adds a pleasant stimulus after a behavior to increase the likelihood that the behavior will recur.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55
Original PDF Rationale
Removing a pleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
An unpleasant condition is removed after performing the desired
behavior
Adding an unpleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
A pleasant stimulus (10 minutes of recreational activity) is added after
performing the desired behavior (fixing bed and cleaning personal
space).

B — Correct
Negative reinforcement removes an aversive condition after a behavior, thereby increasing the likelihood of that behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q64 and 2027 exam Q82
C — Incorrect

Original PDF Rationale
Removing a pleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
The scenario describes a patient with alcohol use disorder who
drinks alcohol to relieve withdrawal symptoms/unpleasant
conditions (tremors, nausea, irritability, photophobia). This is an
example of negative reinforcement, because the behavior
(drinking alcohol) is strengthened by the removal of an aversive
state (withdrawal symptoms).
Adding an unpleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
A pleasant stimulus (award/recognition) is added after performing the
desired behavior (handwashing). Positive reinforcement increases the
likelihood of the behavior being repeated by providing a reward.

Altruism

Direct & Near RepeatsDefense Mechanisms3 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
Altruism is a mature defense in which distress is channeled into constructive service to others. Healthy altruism helps others while preserving care for the self.
Examiner Pattern: The same core fact reappears with very similar wording, a closely related scenario, or only small changes in the answer set.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale
2028 Q88
PDF p. 22
Despite facing personal struggles, a man dedicates much of his time volunteering at a local charity, finding fulfillment in helping others rather than focusing on his problems. Which defense mechanism is he demonstrating?
A. Reaction formation
B. Altruism ✓
C. Projection
D. Displacement
B
A — Incorrect
Reaction formation manages an unacceptable impulse by expressing its opposite or antithetical form.Source basis: Defense p.3
B — Correct
Altruism is a mature defense in which distress is channeled into constructive service to others. Healthy altruism helps others while preserving care for the self.Source basis: Defense p.3
C — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
D — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3
No original rationale was provided in the exam file for this item.

Same Concept, New FramingIHP / Biopsychosocial3 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027

A. Sigmund Freud

D. Carl Jung
C
A — Incorrect

B — Incorrect
George Engel formulated the biopsychosocial model, which understands illness through interacting biological, psychological, and social factors.Source basis: IHP p.1
C — Correct
Jaime Zaguirre performed the first trans-orbital lobotomy in the Philippines in 1949, became UERM Psychiatry chair, and coined the term Individual Human Person.Source basis: History p.4 and IHP p.3

Original PDF Rationale
Proponent of psychoanalytic theory in late 19th and early 20th
centuries.
George Engel introduced the Biopsychosocial Model of Illness in the
1970s, which recognizes that health and illness are influenced by a
complex interplay of biological, psychological, and social factors.
Jaime Zaguirre coined the term “individual human person.”
Carl Jung is a Swiss psychologist and psychiatrist, who is the
proponent of analytical psychology.

Acting Out

Same Concept, New FramingDefense Mechanisms2 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
Acting out expresses unconscious impulses through actions instead of recognizing or verbalizing the associated emotions.
Examiner Pattern: The clinical or theoretical framing changes, but the underlying rule stays the same.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale
2028 Q74
PDF p. 19
A teenager who feels intense anger towards their parents repeatedly breaks household items immediately after arguments, without thinking of its consequences. This defense mechanism is:
A. Reaction Formation
B. Projection
C. Acting Out ✓
D. Displacement
C
A — Incorrect
Reaction formation manages an unacceptable impulse by expressing its opposite or antithetical form.Source basis: Defense p.3
B — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
C — Correct
Acting out expresses unconscious impulses through actions instead of recognizing or verbalizing the associated emotions.Source basis: Defense p.2
D — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3
Original PDF Rationale
Reaction Formation: Transforming an unacceptable wish
or impulse into its opposite
Projection: Perceiving and reacting to unacceptable inner impulses
and their derivatives as though they were outside the self
Acting out: Direct expression of unconscious feelings through
impulsive destructive behavior instead of reflecting on or verbalizing
them.
Displacement: Shifting feelings associated with one idea or object to
another that resembles the original in some way.

George Engel and the Biopsychosocial Model

Direct & Near RepeatsIHP / Biopsychosocial2 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
George Engel formulated the biopsychosocial model, which understands illness through interacting biological, psychological, and social factors.
Examiner Pattern: The same core fact reappears with very similar wording, a closely related scenario, or only small changes in the answer set.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale

PDF p. 5
The proponent of the Biopsychosocial model of Illness:
A. Sigmund Freud
B. George Engel ✓
C. Jaime Zaguirre
D. Carl Jung
B
A — Incorrect
Sigmund Freud developed the psychoanalytic approach, emphasizing unconscious meanings, conflicts, drives, and early life experiences.Source basis: History p.2
B — Correct
George Engel formulated the biopsychosocial model, which understands illness through interacting biological, psychological, and social factors.Source basis: IHP p.1
C — Incorrect
Jaime Zaguirre performed the first trans-orbital lobotomy in the Philippines in 1949, became UERM Psychiatry chair, and coined the term Individual Human Person.Source basis: History p.4 and IHP p.3
D — Incorrect
Carl Jung developed analytical psychology and described the collective unconscious and archetypes such as persona, shadow, anima/animus, and Self.Source basis: 2028/2027 personality exam rationales
Original PDF Rationale
Introduced psychoanalysis
Introduced in the biopsychosocial model in the 1970s
Coined the term “individual human person”
Improved on Sigmund Freud’s theory

Paternalistic / Autocratic Doctor-Patient Model

Same Concept, New FramingIHP / Biopsychosocial2 appearancesYears: 2028, 2027
⌄
Master Rule
In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.
Examiner Pattern: The clinical or theoretical framing changes, but the underlying rule stays the same.
SourceQuestion and Complete ChoicesKeyChoice-by-Choice Rationale

In this type of doctor-patient interaction, the doctor is assumed to know best especially during critical, life-and-death situations
A. Paternalistic ✓
B. Informative
C. Collaborative
D. Relationship-centered
A
A — Correct
In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.Source basis: IHP p.2
B — Incorrect
In the informative model, the physician freely provides information and leaves the choice entirely to the patient, often in one-time consultations without an established relationship.Source basis: IHP p.3
C — Incorrect
The doctor freely dispenses information, but the choice of action is left entirely to the patient Not a type of doctor-patient interaction This model emphasizes a collaborative approach where the doctor and the patient work together, blending the physician's knowledge and expertise with the patient's unique perspective, needs, and assessment of outcomes…Source basis: Original uploaded exam rationale
D — Incorrect
Relationship-centered or patient-centered care treats patients as partners and blends physician expertise with the patient’s perspective, needs, and preferences through collaborative dialogue.Source basis: IHP p.3

In this model, the doctor knows best, and the patient is expected to
comply without questioning. This approach is particularly applicable
in emergency situations or when the patient is overwhelmed or
psychologically incapable of making decisions, such as in cases of
severe mood symptoms, florid psychosis, or severe suicidality.

affecting it
Paternalistic (Autocratic): Doctor prescribes treatment, and the patient is expected
to comply without questioning. Desirable in emergency situations. Doctor knows
best.
Interpretive: Physician and patient share the decision making. Doctor

suggestions.

2028 Q2 · This German psychiatrist made the first medical report of catatonia and used the term cyclothymia to describe mania and depression as stages of the…

History of PsychiatryKey CPDF p. 1
⌄
This German psychiatrist made the first medical report of catatonia and used the term cyclothymia to describe mania and depression as stages of the same illness:
A. Benedict Morel
B. Wilhelm Griesinger
C. Karl Kahlbaum ✓
D. Sigmund Freud

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Benedict Morel reported an early case of schizophrenic psychosis in 1852 and used the term démence précoce for deteriorating illness beginning in adolescence.Source basis: History p.2
B — Incorrect
Griesinger later described cases that were likely schizophrenia, after Morel’s earlier report.Source basis: History p.2
C — Correct
Karl Kahlbaum made the first medical case report of catatonia in 1874 and later used cyclothymia for mania and depression as stages of the same illness.Source basis: History p.2
D — Incorrect
Sigmund Freud developed the psychoanalytic approach, emphasizing unconscious meanings, conflicts, drives, and early life experiences.Source basis: History p.2
Original PDF Rationale

The psychosocial model was developed by Walter Menninger and
other colleagues. It suggests that all psychiatric disorders could be

A. Parasympathetic-Vagal Axis
B. Limbic-Hippocampal Axis

D. Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) Axis

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Parasympathetic activation promotes relaxation. Slow diaphragmatic breathing lowers heart rate and blood pressure through parasympathetic activity.Source basis: Stress p.3
B — Incorrect
Sympathetic-Adrenal-Medullary (SAM) AxisSource basis: Original uploaded exam rationale
C — Correct
The SAM axis mediates the fast fight-or-flight response: hypothalamus activates the sympathetic system, the adrenal medulla releases epinephrine and norepinephrine, and heart rate, blood pressure, glucose, and alertness rise.Source basis: Stress p.1
D — Incorrect
The HPA axis is the slower sustained stress pathway: CRH → ACTH → adrenal cortical glucocorticoids, mainly cortisol.Source basis: Stress p.1
Original PDF Rationale
Sympathetic-Adrenal-Medullary (SAM) Axis
● “Fight or flight” (fast response)
● Hypothalamus → activates sympathetic nervous system
● Adrenal medulla releases:
○ Epinephrine (adrenaline)
○ Norepinephrine (noradrenaline)
● Effects: ↑ HR, NP, glucose, alertness

Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) Axis
Stressor → hypothalamus releases corticotropin-releasing hormone
(CRH) → prompts pituitary gland to release adrenocorticotropic hormone
(ACTH) → Glucocorticoids (Cortisol)
CORTISOL helps the body to cope with prolonged or chronic stress by:
● Mobilizing energy stores
● Suppress inflammation and immune responses so it would be
able to support the body in terms of adaptation to challenges

2028 Q9 · Which type of stress is considered positive and can motivate performance?

Which type of stress is considered positive and can motivate performance?
A. Distress
B. Chronic Stress
C. Eustress ✓
D. Episodic Acute Stress

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Distress is harmful or overwhelming stress that impairs coping.Source basis: Stress p.1
B — Incorrect
Chronic stress is long-term and harmful, with risks including burnout and physical and mental health problems.Source basis: Stress p.1
C — Correct
Eustress is positive short-term stress that can motivate performance and build resilience.Source basis: Stress p.1
D — Incorrect
Episodic acute stress is recurrent or repeated acute stress and may become maladaptive, with risks such as anxiety and hypertension.Source basis: Stress p.1

A. Resilience ✓
B. Post-traumatic growth
C. Stress tolerance
D. Grit

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct

B — Incorrect
The 2028 exam distinguishes post-traumatic growth as positive psychological change following trauma, rather than the broader process of adapting well during adversity.Source basis: 2028 exam Q14
C — Incorrect
The 2028 exam describes stress tolerance as the ability to endure stress. It is narrower than resilience, which emphasizes adaptive recovery and functioning.Source basis: 2028 exam Q14
D — Incorrect
The stress lecture includes grit as intentional, purpose-rooted goal setting used to cultivate resilience.Source basis: Stress p.3

2028 Q15 · Deep breathing directly activates which system?

A. Sympathetic nervous system
B. Parasympathetic nervous system ✓
C. Endocrine system
D. Limbic system

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect

B — Correct

C — Incorrect

Slow diaphragmatic breathing lowers heart rate and blood pressure through parasympathetic activity.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
D — Incorrect

Original PDF Rationale
Deep breathing directly activates the parasympathetic system. It lowers
blood pressure, heart rate, and encourages relaxation by activating the
vagus nerve. This promotes a more tranquil physiological state by
counteracting the effects of the sympathetic activity.

2028 Q17 · Which disorder involves emotional or behavioral symptoms that develop within three months of an identifiable stressor, cause significant impairment,…

Stress ManagementKey APDF p. 4
⌄
Which disorder involves emotional or behavioral symptoms that develop within three months of an identifiable stressor, cause significant impairment, and resolve within six months after the stressor ends?
A. Adjustment disorder ✓
B. Generalized anxiety disorder
C. Post-traumatic stress disorder
D. Major depressive disorder

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
The exam describes adjustment disorder as emotional or behavioral symptoms tied to an identifiable stressor, beginning within three months and resolving within six months after the stressor ends.Source basis: 2028 exam Q17
B — Incorrect
The exam describes generalized anxiety disorder by excessive, difficult-to-control worry.Source basis: 2028 exam Q17
C — Incorrect
PTSD follows severe or traumatic stress and is characterized in the exam rationale by intrusive memories, avoidance, hyperarousal, and emotional numbing.Source basis: 2028 exam Q17
D — Incorrect
The exam links major depression with sadness, hopelessness, and loss of interest or anhedonia.Source basis: 2028 exam Q17
Original PDF Rationale
Occurs when emotional or behavioral symptoms arise in response to
a significant life stressor like a breakup, job loss, or illness. Individuals
are usually time-limited and tied directly to the stressor.
When a person experiences excessive, uncontrollable worry.
Emerge in response to feelings of sadness, hopelessness, and lack of
interest or anhedonia.
Severe or traumatic stress such as abuse, violence, or disasters can
lead to PTSD. Characterized by intrusive memories, avoidance
symptoms, hyperarousal, and emotional numbness, long after the
traumatic event has passed.

2028 Q23 · The proponent of the Biopsychosocial model of Illness:

IHP / BiopsychosocialKey BPDF p. 5
⌄
The proponent of the Biopsychosocial model of Illness:
A. Sigmund Freud
B. George Engel ✓
C. Jaime Zaguirre
D. Carl Jung

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Sigmund Freud developed the psychoanalytic approach, emphasizing unconscious meanings, conflicts, drives, and early life experiences.Source basis: History p.2
B — Correct
George Engel formulated the biopsychosocial model, which understands illness through interacting biological, psychological, and social factors.Source basis: IHP p.1
C — Incorrect
Jaime Zaguirre performed the first trans-orbital lobotomy in the Philippines in 1949, became UERM Psychiatry chair, and coined the term Individual Human Person.Source basis: History p.4 and IHP p.3
D — Incorrect
Carl Jung developed analytical psychology and described the collective unconscious and archetypes such as persona, shadow, anima/animus, and Self.Source basis: 2028/2027 personality exam rationales
Original PDF Rationale
Introduced psychoanalysis
Introduced in the biopsychosocial model in the 1970s
Coined the term “individual human person”
Improved on Sigmund Freud’s theory

2028 Q25 · This type of doctor-patient interaction makes patients their partners in collaborating and co-managing their health.

IHP / BiopsychosocialKey DPDF p. 6
⌄
This type of doctor-patient interaction makes patients their partners in collaborating and co-managing their health.
A. Paternalistic
B. Informative
C. Autocratic
D. Relationship-centered ✓

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.Source basis: IHP p.2
B — Incorrect
In the informative model, the physician freely provides information and leaves the choice entirely to the patient, often in one-time consultations without an established relationship.Source basis: IHP p.3
C — Incorrect
In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.Source basis: IHP p.2
D — Correct
Relationship-centered or patient-centered care treats patients as partners and blends physician expertise with the patient’s perspective, needs, and preferences through collaborative dialogue.Source basis: IHP p.3
Original PDF Rationale
Paternalistic: Patient is expected to comply without questioning; Doctor
takes control for life-saving decisions
Informative: Doctor dispenses information freely and leaves the choice
to patient
Autocratic is also known as Paternalistic
Relationship-centered: Collaborative dialogue wherein the doctor is the
expert who helps the patient co-manage his health to the extent that is
most comfortable for the person and still most medically sound for the
medical condition
Reference trans/book: 2028 Trans 02 - IHP, Biopsychosocial
Approach to Illness

2028 Q31 · During an interview, the patient expressed sorrow over the passing of a friend. After hearing her story, the psychiatrist responded, “I’m very sorry…

IHP / BiopsychosocialKey DPDF p. 7
⌄
During an interview, the patient expressed sorrow over the passing of a friend. After hearing her story, the psychiatrist responded, “I’m very sorry for your loss. That must have been difficult for you. I understand how close you were to your friend”. This statement is an example of:
A. Clarifying statement
B. Summarizing statement
C. Probing statement
D. Empathic statement ✓

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Clarifying statements check whether the clinician understood the patient correctly and invite correction, supporting bidirectional communication.Source basis: IHP p.3
B — Incorrect
Summarizing condenses and restates information from the patient to confirm shared understanding.Source basis: IHP p.3, collaboration around history-taking
C — Incorrect
Probing seeks additional detail. The IHP lecture emphasizes beginning with open-ended questions and then clarifying as needed.Source basis: IHP p.3
D — Correct
Statements such as “I’m sorry to hear that” or “That must have been difficult for you” are empathic statements that convey understanding of the patient’s feelings.Source basis: IHP p.3

centuries.

Carl Jung is a Swiss psychologist and psychiatrist, who is the

2028 Q35 · In this type of doctor-patient interaction, the doctor is assumed to know best especially during critical, life-and-death situations

IHP / BiopsychosocialKey APDF p. 9
⌄
In this type of doctor-patient interaction, the doctor is assumed to know best especially during critical, life-and-death situations
A. Paternalistic ✓
B. Informative
C. Collaborative
D. Relationship-centered

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.Source basis: IHP p.2
B — Incorrect
In the informative model, the physician freely provides information and leaves the choice entirely to the patient, often in one-time consultations without an established relationship.Source basis: IHP p.3
C — Incorrect
The doctor freely dispenses information, but the choice of action is left entirely to the patient Not a type of doctor-patient interaction This model emphasizes a collaborative approach where the doctor and the patient work together, blending the physician's knowledge and expertise with the patient's unique perspective, needs, and assessment of outcomes…Source basis: Original uploaded exam rationale
D — Incorrect
Relationship-centered or patient-centered care treats patients as partners and blends physician expertise with the patient’s perspective, needs, and preferences through collaborative dialogue.Source basis: IHP p.3
Original PDF Rationale
In this model, the doctor knows best, and the patient is expected to
comply without questioning. This approach is particularly applicable
in emergency situations or when the patient is overwhelmed or
psychologically incapable of making decisions, such as in cases of
severe mood symptoms, florid psychosis, or severe suicidality.
The doctor freely dispenses information, but the choice of action is
left entirely to the patient
Not a type of doctor-patient interaction
This model emphasizes a collaborative approach where the doctor
and the patient work together, blending the physician's knowledge
and expertise with the patient's unique perspective, needs, and
assessment of outcomes

2028 Q41 · According to Freud, which part of the personality operates on the pleasure principle?

Theories of PersonalityKey BPDF p. 10
⌄
According to Freud, which part of the personality operates on the pleasure principle?
A. Ego
B. Id ✓
C. Self
D. Superego

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
The ego is the executive component of the psyche. It operates by the reality principle, controls perception and behavior, mediates among id, superego, and reality, and uses defense mechanisms.Source basis: Defense p.1
B — Correct
The id is the reservoir of instinctual drives, operates on the pleasure principle, uses primary-process thinking, and is entirely unconscious.Source basis: Defense p.1 and 2028 exam Q41
C — Incorrect
In the Jungian archetype material used by the exams, the Self represents unification of conscious and unconscious aspects and individuation/self-actualization.Source basis: 2027 exam Q81
D — Incorrect
The superego represents moral conscience and internalized values. It evaluates thoughts and behavior against internal standards and can generate pride or guilt.Source basis: Defense p.1 and 2028 exam Q48
Original PDF Rationale
Operates on the pleasure principle
Operates on the reality principle
Not included in the structural model of the mind
Operates on the ideal principle

A. Modelling ✓
B. Negative reinforcement
C. Phallic fixations
D. Reinforcement schedules

Choice-by-choice rationale

The exam associates phallic fixation with traits such as vanity, exhibitionism, and pride.Source basis: 2028 exam Q60

B. Humility ✓

2028 Q55 · A patient admitted to the psychiatric ward fixed his bed and cleaned his personal space in the morning. As a result, the nursing staff gave him an…

Theories of PersonalityKey DPDF p. 14
⌄
A patient admitted to the psychiatric ward fixed his bed and cleaned his personal space in the morning. As a result, the nursing staff gave him an additional 10 minutes of recreational activity in the afternoon. This scenario best illustrates which behavioral principle?
A. Negative Punishment
B. Negative Reinforcement
C. Positive Punishment
D. Positive Reinforcement ✓

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Negative punishment removes a pleasant stimulus in order to decrease a behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55 and 2027 exam Q82
B — Incorrect
Negative reinforcement removes an aversive condition after a behavior, thereby increasing the likelihood of that behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q64 and 2027 exam Q82
C — Incorrect
Positive punishment adds an aversive consequence to decrease a behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55 and 2027 exam Q82
D — Correct
Positive reinforcement adds a pleasant stimulus after a behavior to increase the likelihood that the behavior will recur.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55
Original PDF Rationale
Removing a pleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
An unpleasant condition is removed after performing the desired
behavior
Adding an unpleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
A pleasant stimulus (10 minutes of recreational activity) is added after
performing the desired behavior (fixing bed and cleaning personal
space).

2028 Q63 · A veteran soldier experiences jumpiness and panic attacks whenever he hears a loud banging sound, because he associates it with bombs exploding while…

⌄

A. Conditioned Response ✓
B. Conditioned Stimulus
C. Unconditioned Stimulus
D. Unconditioned Response

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
A conditioned response is the learned response elicited by a conditioned stimulus after conditioning.Source basis: 2028 exam Q63
B — Incorrect

C — Incorrect
An unconditioned stimulus naturally elicits a response without prior learning.Source basis: 2028 exam Q63
D — Incorrect
An unconditioned response is the natural, unlearned response to an unconditioned stimulus.Source basis: 2028 exam Q63

2028 Q64 · A person with an alcohol abuse disorder who drinks an alcoholic beverage to alleviate tremulousness, nausea, irritability and photophobia is best…

Theories of PersonalityKey BPDF p. 17
⌄
A person with an alcohol abuse disorder who drinks an alcoholic beverage to alleviate tremulousness, nausea, irritability and photophobia is best described by which of the following?
A. Negative Punishment
B. Negative Reinforcement ✓
C. Positive Punishment
D. Positive Reinforcement

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Negative punishment removes a pleasant stimulus in order to decrease a behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55 and 2027 exam Q82
B — Correct
Negative reinforcement removes an aversive condition after a behavior, thereby increasing the likelihood of that behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q64 and 2027 exam Q82
C — Incorrect
Positive punishment adds an aversive consequence to decrease a behavior.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55 and 2027 exam Q82
D — Incorrect
Positive reinforcement adds a pleasant stimulus after a behavior to increase the likelihood that the behavior will recur.Source basis: 2028 exam Q55
Original PDF Rationale
Removing a pleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
The scenario describes a patient with alcohol use disorder who
drinks alcohol to relieve withdrawal symptoms/unpleasant
conditions (tremors, nausea, irritability, photophobia). This is an
example of negative reinforcement, because the behavior
(drinking alcohol) is strengthened by the removal of an aversive
state (withdrawal symptoms).
Adding an unpleasant stimulus to reduce a behavior
A pleasant stimulus (award/recognition) is added after performing the
desired behavior (handwashing). Positive reinforcement increases the
likelihood of the behavior being repeated by providing a reward.

2028 Q66 · Which does not belong to the Topographical Model of the Mind?

Theories of PersonalityKey CPDF p. 17
⌄
Which does not belong to the Topographical Model of the Mind?
A. Conscious mind
B. Pre-conscious mind
C. Pre-unconscious mind ✓
D. Unconscious mind

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Conscious material is what the person is aware of at the present moment.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42
B — Incorrect
Preconscious material is not currently in awareness but can be brought into consciousness by shifting attention or being prompted.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42 and Q68
C — Correct
“Pre-unconscious” is not one of Freud’s topographic divisions. The exam identifies conscious, preconscious, and unconscious as the three levels.Source basis: 2028 exam Q66
D — Incorrect
Freud’s unconscious contains memories, wishes, instincts, and other material that is repressed or difficult to bring into conscious awareness.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42 and Q67
Original PDF Rationale
The Pre-unconscious mind is NOT part of the topographical map of
the mind

2028 Q69 · This is part of the tripartite structure of the Theory of Mind is described to cover three mental contents:

Defense MechanismsKey BPDF p. 18
⌄

A. Id
B. Ego ✓
C. Superego
D. Unconsciousness

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
The id is the reservoir of instinctual drives, operates on the pleasure principle, uses primary-process thinking, and is entirely unconscious.Source basis: Defense p.1 and 2028 exam Q41
B — Correct
The ego is the executive component of the psyche. It operates by the reality principle, controls perception and behavior, mediates among id, superego, and reality, and uses defense mechanisms.Source basis: Defense p.1
C — Incorrect
The superego represents moral conscience and internalized values. It evaluates thoughts and behavior against internal standards and can generate pride or guilt.Source basis: Defense p.1 and 2028 exam Q48
D — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Ego: The ego is the executive component of the psyche. It operates by the reality principle, controls perception and behavior, mediates among id, superego, and reality, and uses defense mechanisms.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
Original PDF Rationale
Completely unconscious intrapsychic agency, only interested in
discharging tension.
Executive organ of the psyche which controls motility, perception, and
contact with reality. It is part of the tripartite structure, yes, but Id is
more strictly linked with the unconscious level.
Also part of the structure but not as directly associated with the
unconscious as the Id.
Mostly unconscious, but aspects of it are certainly conscious; this
agency incorporates the moral conscience
Part of the Topographical model of the mind by Freud and not the
tripartite structure of the Theory of Mind

2028 Q71 · A man is frequently jealous so he accuses his partner of jealousy without lacking clear evidence. According to psychoanalytic defense mechanism…

Defense MechanismsKey BPDF p. 18
⌄
A man is frequently jealous so he accuses his partner of jealousy without lacking clear evidence. According to psychoanalytic defense mechanism theory, which defense mechanism is he most likely exhibiting?
A. Repression
B. Projection ✓
C. Denial
D. Blocking

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Repression unconsciously blocks unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or memories from awareness. Primary repression prevents material from becoming conscious, while secondary repression removes previously conscious material.Source basis: Defense p.3
B — Correct
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
C — Incorrect
Denial refuses to acknowledge painful or unacceptable external reality despite clear evidence.Source basis: Defense p.2
D — Incorrect
Blocking is temporary inhibition of thoughts, emotions, or impulses. Unlike repression, the person remains aware of inner tension produced by the inhibition.Source basis: Defense p.2
Original PDF Rationale
Expelling unacceptable ideas or impulses or blocking them from
entering consciousness.
Projection involves attributing one’s own
unacknowledged feelings to others
Avoiding awareness of aspects of external reality that are difficult to
face by disregarding sensory data.
An inhibition, usually temporary in nature, of affects especially, but
possibly also thinking and impulses.
📖
Reference trans/book: (2028) Defense Mechanisms

B — Correct
Denial refuses to acknowledge painful or unacceptable external reality despite clear evidence.Source basis: Defense p.2
C — Incorrect
Repression unconsciously blocks unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or memories from awareness. Primary repression prevents material from becoming conscious, while secondary repression removes previously conscious material.Source basis: Defense p.3
D — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3

2028 Q73 · A person experiencing deep grief after losing a loved one begins to have chronic unexplained physical pain and frequently complains of illness,…

Defense MechanismsKey BPDF p. 19
⌄
A person experiencing deep grief after losing a loved one begins to have chronic unexplained physical pain and frequently complains of illness, without an identifiable cause. This is understood as:
A. Projection
B. Somatization ✓
C. Intellectualization
D. Displacement

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
B — Correct
Somatization converts psychological distress into physical symptoms or bodily complaints rather than consciously experiencing the emotion.Source basis: Defense p.2-3
C — Incorrect
Intellectualization controls affect by thinking about the issue instead of emotionally experiencing it, emphasizing facts and logic while detaching feelings.Source basis: Defense p.3
D — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3
Original PDF Rationale
Projection: Attributing own feelings to others
Somatization: Converting emotion into bodily symptoms
Intellectualization: Overanalyzing to avoid feelings
Displacement: Redirecting impulse to safer target

2028 Q74 · A teenager who feels intense anger towards their parents repeatedly breaks household items immediately after arguments, without thinking of its…

Defense MechanismsKey CPDF p. 19
⌄
A teenager who feels intense anger towards their parents repeatedly breaks household items immediately after arguments, without thinking of its consequences. This defense mechanism is:
A. Reaction Formation
B. Projection
C. Acting Out ✓
D. Displacement

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Reaction formation manages an unacceptable impulse by expressing its opposite or antithetical form.Source basis: Defense p.3
B — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
C — Correct
Acting out expresses unconscious impulses through actions instead of recognizing or verbalizing the associated emotions.Source basis: Defense p.2
D — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3
Original PDF Rationale
Reaction Formation: Transforming an unacceptable wish
or impulse into its opposite
Projection: Perceiving and reacting to unacceptable inner impulses
and their derivatives as though they were outside the self
Acting out: Direct expression of unconscious feelings through
impulsive destructive behavior instead of reflecting on or verbalizing
them.
Displacement: Shifting feelings associated with one idea or object to
another that resembles the original in some way.

2028 Q75 · A person who has lost a parent often begins to speak and behave in ways that are very similar to those of that parent, unconsciously adapting their…

Defense MechanismsKey BPDF p. 19
⌄

A. Projection
B. Introjection ✓
C. Displacement
D. Splitting

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
B — Correct

C — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3

Original PDF Rationale
Projection: on a nonpsychotic level, projection involves attributing one’s
own unacknowledged feelings to others.
Introjection: internalizing aspects of a significant person
as a way of dealing with the loss of that person.
Displacement: Shifting feelings associated with one idea
or object to another that resembles the original in some

or impulse into its opposite.

2028 Q82 · How does repression differ from denial?

Defense MechanismsKey BPDF p. 21
⌄
How does repression differ from denial?
A. Repression is a conscious process; denial is unconscious
B. Repression deals with internal thoughts and feelings; denial involves refusing to acknowledge external sensory reality ✓
C. Repression involves distorting reality; denial involves intellectualizing
D. Repression is a form of acting out; denial is sublimation

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Freud’s unconscious contains memories, wishes, instincts, and other material that is repressed or difficult to bring into conscious awareness.Source basis: 2028 exam Q42 and Q67
B — Correct
Repression unconsciously blocks unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or memories from awareness. Primary repression prevents material from becoming conscious, while secondary repression removes previously conscious material.Source basis: Defense p.3
C — Incorrect
Repression unconsciously blocks unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or memories from awareness. Primary repression prevents material from becoming conscious, while secondary repression removes previously conscious material.Source basis: Defense p.3
D — Incorrect
Sublimation channels unacceptable impulses into socially acceptable, constructive, or productive activities.Source basis: Defense p.4
Original PDF Rationale
Both repression and denial are unconscious defense mechanisms.
Saying repression is conscious is wrong because if it were conscious,
it would no longer be repression.
Repression keeps painful thoughts or memories out of awareness
(e.g., forgetting a traumatic event). Denial is refusing to acknowledge
reality (e.g., a smoker refusing to believe smoking causes harm).
Incorrect. Intellectualization is a separate defense mechanism
(focusing on facts/logic instead of emotions). “Distortion” is not the
accurate definition of repression.
Incorrect. Acting out (expressing emotions through actions) and
sublimation (channeling impulses into socially acceptable activities)
are distinct defense mechanisms, not equivalent to repression or
denial.

Galen and Plato are described as taking a categorical approach to disease. Galen focused more directly on the disease itself.Source basis: History p.1

human traits (ęboth normal and abnormaè) as existing on a spectrum, and that the
individual had to be treated with a personalized treatment.
individual had to be treated with a personalized treatment.

2027 Q4 · In 1852, a french psychiatrist made the first medical case report on schizophrenic psychosis “démence précoce”

⌄
In 1852, a french psychiatrist made the first medical case report on schizophrenic psychosis “démence précoce”
A. Benedict Morel ✓
B. Wilhelm Griesinger
C. Karl Kahlbaum
D. Eugene Bleuler

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
Benedict Morel reported an early case of schizophrenic psychosis in 1852 and used the term démence précoce for deteriorating illness beginning in adolescence.Source basis: History p.2
B — Incorrect
Griesinger later described cases that were likely schizophrenia, after Morel’s earlier report.Source basis: History p.2
C — Incorrect
Karl Kahlbaum made the first medical case report of catatonia in 1874 and later used cyclothymia for mania and depression as stages of the same illness.Source basis: History p.2
D — Incorrect
Eugen Bleuler coined schizophrenia and described the fundamental “four As”: associations, affect, autism, and ambivalence.Source basis: History p.2
Original PDF Rationale
Benedict Morel was the one who first used the term to describe deteriorated patients
whose illnesses began in adolescence
Wilhelm Griesinger was the one who described later on what was likely
schizophrenia

Karl Kahlbaum used the term cyclothymia to describe mania and depression as
stages of the same illness

● Increased serum glucose levels
General Adaptation Syndrome was originally described by Hans Selye in the 1920s.
His views of stress are defined as the nonspecific response of the body to any
demand placed upon it to adapt. He described the body’s short term and long term
reaction to stress in three stages: alarm, resistance, exhaustion.

2027 Q42 · In this stage, humans exhibit a “fight or flight” response, as a reaction to stress.

⌄
In this stage, humans exhibit a “fight or flight” response, as a reaction to stress.
A. Stage I ✓
B. Stage II
C. Stage III
D. Stage IV

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
Alarm is the first GAS stage and includes the immediate fight-or-flight response.Source basis: 2027 exam Q42
B — Incorrect
Resistance is the GAS stage in which the body adapts to continuing stressors and attempts to reduce their effects.Source basis: 2027 exam Q43
C — Incorrect
Exhaustion occurs after prolonged stress depletes adaptive resources, increasing physical, psychological, and interpersonal consequences.Source basis: 2027 exam Q44
D — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Stage I: Alarm is the first GAS stage and includes the immediate fight-or-flight response.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
Original PDF Rationale
Stage I: Alarm Reaction
● Immediate response to a stressor, causing anxiety, fear, shock, or
confusion.
● "Fight or flight" response prepares the body for action but weakens the
immune system, making one susceptible to illness.
● Also called the self-correcting stage.
● Also called the self-correcting stage.
Stage II: Resistance (Adaptation)
● Body adapts to ongoing stress, characterized by aggression, withdrawal,
or repression.
● Changes occur to minimize the stressor's effects. For instance, in
starvation, physical activity may decrease, and nutrient absorption may
increase.
increase.
Stage III: Exhaustion
● Prolonged stress leads to physical (headaches, flu), psychological
(depression), and interpersonal (relationship breakdown) effects.
● The body’s defenses collapse, with reduced immunity leading to
vulnerability to serious illnesses like heart attacks or infections.

Catecholamines are released in the rapid stress response. The SAM pathway involves epinephrine and norepinephrine.Source basis: Stress p.1 and 2027 exam Q46
B — Incorrect
The 2027 exam identifies glutamate as an excitatory neurotransmitter that can modulate stress-response circuitry, but it is not the catecholamine released by the noradrenergic system.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
C — Incorrect
The 2027 exam notes that peptides are chains of amino acids and are not the specific stress hormone asked in the glucocorticoid question.Source basis: 2027 exam Q47 rationale
D — Incorrect
Thyroxine is a thyroid hormone primarily involved in metabolism, not the catecholamine or glucocorticoid response tested in these stress questions.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
Original PDF Rationale
Activation of noradrenergic system (specifically Locus ceruleus) → causes
catecholamine release from ANS [2025]
Corticotropin releasing factor, glutamate, GABA generate stress response or
modulate dopaminergic and noradrenergic brain circuitry [2025]
Category of molecules [2025]
Regulates metabolism [2025]

2027 Q75 · A 7 yr old got chased by a dog. To escape the dog, he was able to climb a tree. What gave him the burst of energy to be able to do what he did?

Stress ManagementKey APDF p. 9
⌄
A 7 yr old got chased by a dog. To escape the dog, he was able to climb a tree. What gave him the burst of energy to be able to do what he did?
A. Glucocorticoid ✓
B. Peptides
C. Catecholamines
D. Thyroxine

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
Glucocorticoids such as cortisol are released through the HPA pathway and mobilize energy while helping the body adapt to prolonged stress.Source basis: Stress p.1 and 2027 exam Q47
B — Incorrect
The 2027 exam notes that peptides are chains of amino acids and are not the specific stress hormone asked in the glucocorticoid question.Source basis: 2027 exam Q47 rationale
C — Incorrect
Catecholamines are released in the rapid stress response. The SAM pathway involves epinephrine and norepinephrine.Source basis: Stress p.1 and 2027 exam Q46
D — Incorrect
Thyroxine is a thyroid hormone primarily involved in metabolism, not the catecholamine or glucocorticoid response tested in these stress questions.Source basis: 2027 exam Q46-47 rationale
Original PDF Rationale
Glucocorticoid increases blood sugar levels, providing the burst of energy needed to
respond to stress.
Peptides are short chains of amino acids
Catecholamines also initiate rapid response, but glucocorticoids are for more
complex adaptive responses such as climbing a tree.
Hormone that regulates metabolism. Influences overall energy levels but does not
provide the rapid burst of energy during an acute stress situation.

2027 Q23 · A patient consults the Emergency Room Doctor after the interview and examination. She is prescribed treatment and is expected to comply without…

A. Paternalistic ✓
B. Interpretative
C. Informative
D. Deliberate

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Correct
In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.Source basis: IHP p.2
B — Incorrect
The project materials do not separately define this distractor. For this stem, they specifically identify Paternalistic: In the paternalistic or autocratic model, the doctor is assumed to know best and the patient is expected to comply. It is most applicable to emergencies or situations where the patient cannot make safe decisions.Source basis: Project-source limitation + keyed source concept
C — Incorrect
In the informative model, the physician freely provides information and leaves the choice entirely to the patient, often in one-time consultations without an established relationship.Source basis: IHP p.3
D — Incorrect
The doctor may be seen cold and uncaring. Patient is seen as unrealistically autonomous Deliberate: Doctor acts like a friend/counselor.Source basis: Original uploaded exam rationale
Original PDF Rationale
affecting it
Paternalistic (Autocratic): Doctor prescribes treatment, and the patient is expected
to comply without questioning. Desirable in emergency situations. Doctor knows
best.
Interpretive: Physician and patient share the decision making. Doctor
presents/discusses alternatives – find the best decision, make it together, and the
patient participates. Doctor is flexible and willing to consider criticism and alternative
suggestions.
suggestions.
Informative: The doctor dispenses information. Data is freely given, but the choice
is left wholly up to the patient. Appropriate for one-time consultations. The doctor
may be seen cold and uncaring. Patient is seen as unrealistically autonomous
may be seen cold and uncaring. Patient is seen as unrealistically autonomous
Deliberate: Doctor acts like a friend/counselor. Used by doctors to modify
injurious behavior, e.g., trying to stop smoking and losing weight.

Abraham Maslow established the hierarchy of needs: physiological needs, safety
needs, love and belonging, esteem, and self-actualization.
Carl Jung regarded the psyche as made up of separate but interacting systems:
including the ego, the personal unconscious, and the collective unconscious.
Sigmund Freud developed the psychoanalytic theory where thoughts and behavior
are guided mainly by the unconscious part of the mind. The topographic model of
the mind is divided into three: Conscious, Preconscious, and Unconscious.
Carl Rogers focused on client-centered therapy and created its three concepts:
Unconditional Positive Regard, Genuineness, and Empathetic Understanding.

B — Incorrect
Denial refuses to acknowledge painful or unacceptable external reality despite clear evidence.Source basis: Defense p.2
C — Correct
Acting out expresses unconscious impulses through actions instead of recognizing or verbalizing the associated emotions.Source basis: Defense p.2
D — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
Original PDF Rationale
Regression - reverting back to childhood behaviors as a coping mechanism
Denial - Avoiding awareness of aspects of external reality that are
difficult to face by disregarding sensory data
Projection - Involves perceiving and reacting to unacceptable inner impulses and
their derivatives as though they were outside the self

2027 Q62 · A young man recently had a disagreement w/ his colleague regarding their presentation. Although they have already resolved it, he felt slighted by…

Defense MechanismsKey DPDF p. 7
⌄
A young man recently had a disagreement w/ his colleague regarding their presentation. Although they have already resolved it, he felt slighted by the colleague’s dismissal of his ideas. He then started to submit his work late, forget his tasks, and show up late to work. What type of defense mechanism is this?
A. Distortion
B. Displacement
C. Projection
D. Passive aggressive behavior ✓

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Distortion grossly alters external reality to satisfy internal needs or wishes and may involve grandiosity, delusions, or hallucinations.Source basis: Defense p.2
B — Incorrect
Displacement redirects an emotion or impulse from its original target to a safer substitute while the emotion and basic aim remain the same.Source basis: Defense p.3
C — Incorrect
Projection attributes one’s own unacceptable thoughts, feelings, or impulses to another person or external source.Source basis: Defense p.2
D — Correct
Passive-aggressive behavior expresses aggression indirectly through passivity, procrastination, stubbornness, intentional inefficiency, or turning anger against the self.Source basis: Defense p.2
Original PDF Rationale
Distortion - grossly reshaping the experience of external reality to suit inner needs
(e.g. A person who fails a test might tell themselves the questions were unfair,
distorting reality to protect their self-esteem)
Displacement - shifting feelings associated with one idea or object to another that
resembles the original in some way (e.g. Instead of getting mad at your teacher or
friend, you get mad at someone you are more comfortable with, like your siblings)
Projection - perceiving and reacting to unacceptable inner impulses and their
derivatives as though they were outside the self (e.g. A person who frequently lies
might accuse others of being dishonest, projecting their own behavior onto them)
Passive aggressive behavior - aggression toward an object expressed indirectly and
ineffectively through passivity, masochism, and turning against the self (e.g. There
is a worker given an additional workload by his boss, instead of being honest that it
is quite overwhelming. He might be delaying submission or making small mistakes
on purpose. Because he does not want to address the underlying anger)

2027 Q65 · A young monk vowed to a life of celibacy and isolated himself from social gatherings, he believed that forming close relationships would lead him…

⌄

A. Denial
B. Reaction formation
C. Altruism
D. Asceticism ✓

Choice-by-choice rationale

A — Incorrect
Denial refuses to acknowledge painful or unacceptable external reality despite clear evidence.Source basis: Defense p.2
B — Incorrect
Reaction formation manages an unacceptable impulse by expressing its opposite or antithetical form.Source basis: Defense p.3
C — Incorrect
Altruism is a mature defense in which distress is channeled into constructive service to others. Healthy altruism helps others while preserving care for the self.Source basis: Defense p.3
D — Correct
Asceticism is a mature defense involving renunciation or avoidance of pleasurable impulses, often in the service of values or self-control.Source basis: Defense p.4
Original PDF Rationale
Asceticism
● Attempting to eliminate pleasurable aspects of experience because of
internal conflicts produced by that pleasure.
● Can be in the service of transcended or spiritual goals as in celibacy
